> Scope: Developer onboarding, production readiness, and operational handoff for the Asset Management System.

## Current Production Readiness Status

- Simple repair records (2026-10-07, branch `feat/simple-repair-records`, design `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-simple-repair-records-design.md`, staff guide `docs/17_REPAIR_RECORD_GUIDE_TH.md`) replace the multi-step repair ticket workflow with a one-form repair log on the existing `maintenance_tickets`/`maintenance_plans` tables. Record statuses are `in_progress` / `closed` / `cancelled`; old `open`/`reported`/`accepted`/`waiting_parts`/`waiting_vendor`/`completed` rows count as `in_progress` (`openRepairRecordWhere`). New nullable column `outcome` holds `usable` or `beyond_repair`. Asset effects live in `src/lib/repair-record-policy.ts` and `src/lib/repair-record-service.ts`: unfinished → `Under Maintenance`; "ซ่อมเสร็จ" or cancel → custody-derived `In Use`/`Ready` (`In Use` whenever an active checkout exists); `beyond_repair` → `Pending Disposal`; finished/usable on a working asset leaves the status unchanged; every change is a conditional `statusId` claim with `AssetMovement` and System Log. Check-in result "ส่งซ่อม" opens an unfinished record. PM plans are 7-day reminders ("PM ถึงกำหนด") recorded with "บันทึกว่าทำแล้ว", which moves `nextDueDate` by one interval with month-end clamping (fixes review finding B9); PM records are identified only by `maintenancePlanId` (fixes B5); disposal execution ends the asset's plans. Removed: status-step buttons, board, assignee/due-date planning, SLA/overdue alerts, close checklist, the maintenance close approval (`workflow_approval_maintenance_close_required`), automatic PM work orders (`pm:generate-due`, `/api/maintenance-plans/generate-due`, the PM job in `scheduler:heartbeat`, `MAINTENANCE_PM_GENERATION_TOKEN`, `pm_auto_generation_*`). Old setting rows and columns stay in the database; `retiredSystemSettingKeys` hides the settings. Manual migration `prisma/manual-migrations/2026-10-07-add-maintenance-outcome.sql` is applied to `asset_management_dev` only and is **pending for Production** (verified backup + approval first). `npm test` 1,368 tests: 1,367 passed, 0 failed, 1 skipped; `npx tsc --noEmit` and ESLint clean.
- Workflow round 2 (2026-10-07, branch `fix/security-round-1`, commits `9bf2cdd`, `edefbc5`, `56abb27`, `3004555`, `e1a4346`) closes findings A1–A4 and B1–B4 in `docs/audits/2026-10-07-full-review.md`: Checkout, Transfer and legacy return backfill claim the asset with a conditional status update inside the transaction (409 `ASSET_CHANGED_DURING_OPERATION` for the losing concurrent request) and manual migration `2026-10-07-add-active-checkout-unique-index.sql` adds `UX_asset_checkouts_active_assetId` (applied to `asset_management_dev` only, **pending for Production**); bulk update/move and the register edit form can no longer change custody of assets with an open checkout or of Disposed/Retired assets, and bulk custodian assignment moves `Ready` to `In Use`; corrective maintenance claims the asset before counting open tickets, accepts `Ready`/`In Use`/`Pending Repair` without a ticket, and check-in creates repair tickets as `reported` (legacy `open` tickets may now be accepted) — the maintenance parts of this round are superseded by the simple repair records bullet above; `Pending Disposal` assets without an open request can get a disposal request whose rejection restores custody-derived `In Use`/`Ready`; with segregation of duties on, disposal approvers must be linked to an employee and execution is refused when no approver employee was recorded; scan-time audit corrections require `audit:approve` and segregation switched off, otherwise the finding stays pending (`correctionsDeferred`). `npm test` 1,362 passed / 1 skipped; `npx tsc --noEmit` and scoped ESLint clean; bulk update/move rejections verified against the running app on the dev database.
- Security round 1 (2026-10-07, branch `fix/security-round-1`, commits `160505b`, `0bef806`, `276fa57`, `5f83e24`) closes the Critical/High findings in `docs/audits/2026-10-07-full-review.md`: admin user APIs never load or return `passwordHash`; `ldap_bind_password` is masked as `__STORED_SECRET__` in the settings API/page (saving the placeholder keeps the stored value) and redacted in System Log snapshots; only `system_admin` may change `ldap_*` settings, run the LDAP connection test, or apply an LDAP sync from the web, and the test never sends the stored password to a different URL/bind DN; non-admins cannot grant `system_admin` or permissions they do not hold, cannot edit/reset users with more access, and the last active system administrator cannot be demoted or deactivated; JWT sessions re-read active state, active roles and permissions at most every 60 seconds (immediately after an admin edits that user or any role) and inactive roles grant nothing; failed logins lock a username after 5 failures and an IP after 20 failures within 15 minutes (`code=rate_limited`). `npm test` passed 1320/1320 with `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. Existing `system_logs` rows written before this change may still contain the old bind password.
- A repair record can be cancelled ("ยกเลิกบันทึก", optional reason) only while it is unfinished, with optimistic concurrency. Cancellation keeps the record as an auditable `cancelled` row, writes a `maintenance_cancel` movement and System Log entry, restores the asset to the custody-derived `In Use` or `Ready`, and drops it from unfinished-record queries and blockers. Finished records cannot be cancelled; their details are edited instead. Legacy Check-in candidates include `Ready` and `In Use` assets with a current custodian, no open checkout, and no unfinished repair record; this repairs imported/current-custodian return flows without direct lifecycle edits.
- Manual SQL migration tracking is implemented through `manual_migration_history` and the `migration:init`, `migration:status`, `migration:apply`, and `migration:baseline` CLI commands. The ledger stores exact SHA-256 checksums, target database, operator, reason, duration, and success/failed/baselined history; status is read-only, changed accepted files are blocked, one migration runs under a SQL Server application lock, and sensitive connection values are sanitized. The `asset_management` ledger was initialized after a verified backup on 2026-08-27, and the ten migrations present at that checkpoint were verified and recorded as applied/baselined. The later `2026-08-27-add-reversible-asset-transactions.sql` was applied separately on 2026-08-28 and is now tracked as applied. Since 2026-10-10 (`014f0eb`) every migration command accepts `--db-env <file>`: connection settings then come only from that file, so `.env` stays on the dev database and Production commands use the git-ignored `.env.prod-admin` (see `docs/03_DATABASE.md`).
- Reversible asset transactions are implemented for Check-out, Check-in, and Transfer. New documents capture versioned authoritative before/after asset and installed-component snapshots. A user with `asset:edit` may cancel only the latest active transaction when asset/component state still matches and no downstream work exists; cancellation is serializable and optimistic-concurrency guarded, restores the exact snapshot, keeps the document as `void`, writes a compensating movement/System Log, and reopens a voided Check-in's Checkout. Blocked or legacy snapshot-less attempts do not mutate master data and upsert `transaction_cancellation_blocked` into the Asset State Review queue. Asset Detail exposes one contextual action inside More actions, and print documents preserve VOID metadata/watermark. After a verified backup and explicit approval, `prisma/manual-migrations/2026-08-27-add-reversible-asset-transactions.sql` was applied to `asset_management` on 2026-08-28 with accepted checksum prefix `53f0d2d5bd11`. Post-apply verification found all 16 Checkout/Check-in columns, the Transfer table, six expected indexes, its foreign key, and no duplicate active Check-in; four legacy Check-outs and two legacy Check-ins remain active with null snapshots by design.
- Asset State Governance is implemented with canonical lifecycle/condition policy, workflow-owned transitions, prior-status restoration for rejected disposal, and the `asset_state_reviews` review queue under `Admin > Data Quality`. Detection is review-first and does not automatically change existing asset states. Resolution is issue-specific, reasoned, stale-snapshot guarded, transactional, and audited; dismissal never mutates the asset. `Under Inspection` is an active controlled operational status, remains blocked from generic create/edit and normal checkout/transfer, can be corrected to `Ready` with a reason after inspection, and is no longer emitted as `controlled_legacy_status`; only `Reserved` and `In Transit` retain that detector classification. The 2026-08-27 production rescan covered 1,748 active assets, auto-closed 191 obsolete inspection findings, left 110 pending findings including one true controlled-legacy item, and verified the asset status/condition/update snapshot was unchanged. The verified production database already has `2026-08-27-add-asset-state-governance.sql` and `2026-08-27-keep-under-inspection-operational.sql` applied/baselined in the manual migration ledger; do not rerun them. Prisma Client regeneration and the dedicated UAT items remain deployment verification steps.
- Checkout and Check-in condition dropdowns now use the same canonical seven-condition policy as Asset Create/Edit. Legacy active values such as `Excellent` and `Poor` remain readable on existing records but cannot be selected as a new checkout/check-in condition; both APIs enforce the same rule server-side.
- Sensitive values must stay out of committed docs and source files. Use placeholders such as `<DB_SERVER>`, `<DB_INSTANCE>`, `<DB_USER>`, `<DB_NAME>`, `<DB_PASSWORD>`, `<DB_TLS_SERVER_NAME>`, and `<CHANGE_ME>`.
- Real environment files must stay outside Git. The repository ignores `.env*`; production uses an environment file managed on the server.
- Read-only external-system API integration is available under `/api/integrations/v1`. Phase 1 adds hashed Bearer-token client auth, scoped access helpers, request IDs, audit logging, and `GET /api/integrations/v1/health`. Phase 2 adds `GET /api/integrations/v1/assets` and `GET /api/integrations/v1/assets/{assetTag}` under `asset:read`, with filters for Asset Tag, Serial Number, current custodian employee code, company, branch, current location, status, and condition. Phase 3 adds `GET /api/integrations/v1/assets/changes?updatedSince=...` for bounded incremental sync with `nextCursor`/`highWaterMark`, plus `reference:read` endpoints for statuses, companies, branches, and locations. Phase 4 adds authenticated `GET /api/integrations/v1/openapi`, `npm run integration:token`, and `docs/13_INTEGRATION_API.md` for setup/UAT/production handoff. The DB-backed token manager stores clients in `integration_api_clients` and exposes create/edit display name and scopes/rotate/disable/enable in `Admin > Integration API` (`/{locale}/admin/integrations`) through `/api/admin/integration-clients`. The client list now includes read-only operations UX derived from existing `system_logs` integration API entries: 24-hour/7-day request counts, 7-day errors, top endpoint, latest error, and a scope-aware Copy PowerShell action that uses a token placeholder rather than exposing secrets. Scope expansion requires confirmation because existing tokens gain the newly allowed endpoint immediately. Client lifecycle changes are audited with safe old/new values, and read-only API calls audit route, status, request ID, query/target metadata, and bounded response counts without logging tokens or payloads. `2026-06-14-add-integration-api-clients.sql` is already recorded as applied in the `asset_management` migration ledger; a different target database must apply it after backup/approval before deploy/use. Existing `.env` database connection settings remain unchanged; normal token lifecycle work requires no `.env` update. Store only SHA-256 token hashes and previews in the app, do not reuse user sessions or scheduler tokens for external systems, and keep integration DTOs free of purchase/accounting/supplier/evidence fields unless a future explicit scope is designed. `docs/13_INTEGRATION_API.md` now includes the endpoint summary, employee-custody lookup examples using `employeeCode`, incremental sync, reference data, error handling, client operations UX, and PowerShell guidance for quoted query-string URLs and `curl.exe` versus `Invoke-RestMethod`.
- The system has production-oriented modules for RBAC, audit trail, readiness checks, backup status signals, scheduler heartbeat, LDAP sync safety threshold, PM due reminders, storage governance, and guarded test-data cleanup.
- Dashboard repair lifecycle metrics use exact active Asset Status master rows instead of text matching. `Pending Repair` and `Under Maintenance` are displayed as separate KPI/status-overview entries, and each drilldown opens Asset Register with the exact `statusId`; the maintenance page is the repair-record list and is not used as the asset-status drilldown (the separate "ยังซ่อมไม่เสร็จ" card counts unfinished records and opens `/maintenance?status=in_progress`). The 2026-08-27 database diagnostic snapshot contained 8 active `Pending Repair` assets and 2 active `Under Maintenance` assets (`SNI-EQU-20-0273`, `SNI-EQU-22-0085`). These counts are operational data and will change as workflows progress. No schema or migration is required.
- QR label printing now uses configurable 12/18/24/custom tape profiles, warns before printing if the QR target is localhost/private/relative, and the `/q/a/{assetId}` resolver redirects through Public QR Base URL or forwarded proxy headers to avoid localhost redirects behind Nginx/Cloudflare. The `/th/asset-management/labels` queue supports operational filters for print status, company, branch, category, location, created date, and print ordering; queue actions summarize whether the operator is adding labels by all assets, company, branch, or a specific location so users can print by company/branch without drilling into a long location list. Location options are shown hierarchically by company/branch to disambiguate repeated names, and the empty search guidance is a compact hint under the search box instead of a large placeholder panel. Label queue display text is de-duplicated so assets whose names already include the brand/model do not render repeated strings such as `Monitor ASUS... / ASUS / ASUS...`. Label templates include `{labelAssetName}` for print-only compact asset names; `Admin > Settings > Label / QR` exposes a two-choice `ชื่อทรัพย์สินบน Label` segmented control (`ย่ออัตโนมัติ` / `ชื่อเต็ม`) that can strip the category prefix from suggested asset names on labels without changing `Asset.name` or the full `{assetName}` token. The active label-name choice uses the primary selected-state color and checkmark so operators can see the current mode quickly. The print pages pass the tape-size placeholder through `next-intl` before client-side replacement so Brother guidance no longer throws a missing `tapeSize` formatting error.
- Mobile QR/barcode scanner controls now prefer the rear camera through the browser `environment` camera constraint. Asset scanner inputs can expose camera choice when useful, while Audit Scan intentionally defaults to rear-camera walking mode without a visible camera selector. When multiple camera devices are reported, scanner helpers prefer the generic environment-facing camera instead of auto-selecting a named multi-lens device such as iPhone Back Triple Camera; explicit user camera choices still use the selected device where that UI exists. The dedicated `/th/asset-management/scan` page is QR-first for printed Asset Labels, reads `/q/a/{assetId}` resolver URLs directly, and auto-opens the asset detail page from the scanner success callback after stopping the camera. Asset Label QR mode uses an undistorted 4:3 camera preview with a custom square QR guidance overlay, requests high-resolution 4096x3072/30fps ideal constraints plus continuous focus/exposure/white-balance constraints where supported, and best-effort applies the camera track's maximum reported resolution after the stream opens. Supported Asset QR camera tracks expose flashlight plus bounded base/1x, 2x, and 3x zoom controls in the scanner preview so users can enlarge small labels and return to the unzoomed preview without restarting the camera; unsupported browsers/devices simply hide unavailable controls. Asset QR decoding bypasses the `html5-qrcode` camera loop for a shared direct `getUserMedia` + ZXing `BrowserQRCodeReader.decode(video)` path. This keeps decoding on the native-resolution `video.videoWidth/video.videoHeight` frame instead of the library's CSS-pixel hidden canvas, avoids native `BarcodeDetector` and mirror-flip retry gaps on mobile, and avoids CSS video stretching/object-fill so QR modules are not warped. Serial Number inputs now use the same native-resolution video path with ZXing `BrowserMultiFormatReader`, optional browser `BarcodeDetector` fallback, focused scan-band canvas crops decoded through direct ZXing `ImageData`/`BinaryBitmap` fallback before full-frame decode, a narrower 16:9 barcode guidance overlay with scan line, 1920x1080/30fps camera constraints, and common QR/1D/2D barcode formats for manufacturer labels.
- Audit scan keeps the audit workflow on `/th/audit/rounds/{id}/scan` while using the same native-resolution Asset QR decoder in continuous mode: matched QR scans populate the main input with a readable Asset Tag/label instead of the raw QR URL, while the latest raw QR value remains available in the scanner utility panel for traceability after a decode. Manual entry is separate from QR scanning: typing at least two characters shows suggestion cards from the current round by Asset Tag, label, expected location, custodian, or department. The QR path remains exact-match/raw-value based; partial matching is manual-only, and multiple manual matches require the auditor to choose a row instead of auto-selecting.
- Audit scan field UX now shows the selected asset's system data before the auditor decides whether the field data is correct: expected location, custodian, department, and condition are visible beside the scan result. Fast mode uses explicit post-scan actions: `บันทึกพบตรง`, `บันทึกข้อมูลไม่ตรง / Finding`, `ถ่ายรูปหลักฐาน`, and `สแกนต่อ / กรอกเอง`. Matched scans save immediately and photos are optional. Mismatch opens the detailed actual-value fields; when actual field data differs from expected data, at least one evidence photo is required before save. Evidence photos are free-form and can be queued as multiple files, with an optional tag such as `รูปหลักฐานทั่วไป`, category checklist labels, Serial Number, or Asset Tag for grouping. Scan result wording now separates assets found in the round, assets found in the system but outside the round (`Out-of-scope`), unknown QR values (`ไม่พบทรัพย์สินในระบบ`), and `Found Later` recovery for items previously marked not found. `Mark Not Found` remains a pending-list action for expected audit items that the auditor physically cannot find; it is not shown as a primary action after a successful scan.
- Audit scan out-of-scope detection uses the dedicated `POST /api/audit-rounds/{id}/scan-lookup` route before showing an unknown-asset state. The route requires `audit:edit` and resolves normalized QR/manual candidates against `Asset.id`, `assetTag`, `serialNumber`, and `fixedAssetCode`, then checks `audit_items` for the current round. This avoids relying on permission-aware Global Search (`/api/search`) for audit work and fixes printed `/q/a/{assetId}` labels that belong to assets outside the round. Lookup is read-only: saving an out-of-scope finding still happens only when the user confirms through the existing scan save route, which creates the reviewable `out_of_scope` audit item/finding/history. Out-of-scope scan save can now capture actual location, custodian, department, and condition immediately; changed actual fields create reviewable field-specific findings with linked evidence attachments, and master asset data still changes only through approved finding review. Out-of-scope lookup responses also carry installed component context, so the scan page shows component rows for parent assets outside the round; auditors can scan each component QR from that panel, while confirm-with-parent and missing actions stay disabled when the component has no audit item in the current round.
- Audit component asset support is implemented for parent-led counting. Audit round preview/create expands selected parent assets with active installed `AssetComponent` children before creating `audit_items`, while preserving one audit item per asset. Audit Scan shows installed components under the selected parent, supports direct component scans, and supports explicit `confirmed_with_parent` component confirmation for components that cannot be scanned directly. Parent register edits can sync branch/current location/custodian/department to active installed components through `syncInstalledComponentsWithParent`; transfer/checkout/checkin/bulk operations sync only the fields those workflows change, with component movement rows for every changed component. Audit correction sync applies only to already found or `confirmed_with_parent` components in the round: scan correction syncs location/custodian, and approved findings sync location/custodian/department. Audit branch, condition, and status changes never sync automatically.
- Audit Scan Phase 2 mobile layout is implemented without changing business logic: `/th/audit/rounds/{id}/scan` now uses the sticky progress header as the single progress source for pending/scanned counts, shows photo queue as a small header badge, removes the duplicated desktop/mobile metric-card row, suppresses the disabled submit bar before an item is selected, and shows a fixed bottom action bar after an asset is selected with `บันทึกพบตรง`, `บันทึกข้อมูลไม่ตรง / Finding`, `ถ่ายรูปหลักฐาน`, and `สแกนต่อ / กรอกเอง`. Normal "camera status / ready" text and camera selector controls are hidden to keep the scan panel compact; camera guidance appears only when the scanner is active, a decoded QR reference exists, or the browser reports a camera issue. Desktop keeps the existing inline decision panel and submit behavior.
- Audit Scan Phase 2 follow-up keeps the scan entry as the first focused work area before an item is selected, with a highlighted scan/manual panel and short helper copy. Manual input now supports partial suggestions in the scan panel so auditors can type a fragment such as part of an Asset Tag/name/location/custodian and choose from a bounded list without opening the full picker; exact QR scans still bypass this partial-search path. The sticky header now exposes a `คิวรอตรวจ {count}` quick action that opens a collapsible inline pending queue preview from the existing `audit_items` in the same round; auditors can select a pending item to load its expected data or open the existing pending list for not-found handling. The pending list preserves the audit-scan `returnTo` target so `กลับ` returns to the same scan workflow instead of dropping the user on the round detail page. This is navigation/selection UX only and does not create a new audit workflow or change scan/save/finding/offline logic.
- Adaptive Audit Scan workspace consolidation keeps one `audit-qr-reader` camera subtree and the existing target lock/decoder/constraints. Controller browser QA observed one primary and one supporting region with no body overflow at 375/390/414/768/1280/1440; the 375px Recent Scans header is readable at 284px by 86px, and desktop uses the same nodes in a two-column form grid at 1280px. The initial mobile action bar is absent until an in-round target is selected; at 375/390/414 exactly one mobile action region shows matched, mismatch/Finding, and change/rescan text only, while the same hook is hidden at 768px and above. Mobile content reserves 156px (0 at `md+`), and safe-area action padding computed to 12px in the controller environment. An in-app browser camera attempt created exactly one `#audit-qr-reader` but received no video stream or permission, so camera/torch/zoom are not passed. The reviewer approved after 158 focused tests, scoped ESLint, TypeScript, production build, and diff check. Real-device Android/iPhone camera, torch/zoom, offline-photo retry, keyboard, rotation, and safe-area checks remain manual. The later UI hardening clears `scanFeedback` whenever an audit target is changed or cleared, so a prior out-of-scope notice no longer leaks into the next in-round decision.
- Audit Scan Phase 1 field-device polish keeps Fast Scan Mode and Continuous Scan enabled by default for walking audits but hides the mode switches from the normal UI to reduce field-user confusion. The active scan-result card now focuses only on the current QR/manual outcome, while `สแกนล่าสุด` is a separate compact panel that keeps the newest three reads visible and older reads collapsible with matched, mismatch, out-of-scope, unknown, found-later, saved, or offline-queued status. The old full asset dropdown is replaced by a collapsible searchable picker that defaults to pending `audit_items` but can search all items in the same round by Asset Tag, label, expected location, custodian, or department. Pending queue cards show expected location/custodian/department context so auditors can choose the right item without opening the full list; not-found handling still stays in the pending list/zone queue. Supported mobile camera tracks expose flashlight and 1x/2x/3x zoom controls in the scanner preview, with 1x returning to the unzoomed camera view; each control is hidden when the browser/device does not report the corresponding camera capability, and flashlight turns off when scanning stops or the camera changes. This is UI/device control only and does not change audit item, finding, offline queue, raw QR traceability, permission, or SOD behavior.
- Audit Rounds list is action-first for high-volume counting: `/th/audit/rounds` now shows a next-action panel for `สแกนต่อ`, `Review รายการไม่ตรง`, and `รอบที่พร้อมปิด`, plus URL-backed quick filters (`view=all|open|pending|review|mismatch|readyToClose`) that preserve the dashboard scroll position when toggled. Per-round badges indicate whether the round is ready to close or still blocked by pending audit items or review follow-ups. This is a UI/workflow navigation improvement only; audit scan, finding review, SOD, and close-round guard logic remain unchanged.
- Audit round cancellation is now an explicit read-only lifecycle state (`cancelled`). Users with audit approval permission can cancel a round with a required reason and the cancelled-by user is shown on the historical detail; the system preserves audit items, scan history, findings, evidence, system logs, and any approved/immediate master-data changes instead of rolling them back. Cancelled rounds stay visible for historical review but are excluded from current coverage, operational queues, approval inbox close readiness, scan/pending entry points, mutation routes, and active exports.
- Audit Findings at `/th/audit/findings` is the existing `รายการไม่ตรง` workflow with Phase 2 review polish, not a separate center. Summary cards still show pending review, open actions, overdue actions, and closed findings; quick filters are URL-backed and also drive Excel/PDF exports through the shared `buildAuditFindingWhere` helper; each row shows a clearer system-vs-found comparison plus a resolution-state badge. The page now keeps normal freshness feedback as a quiet `loaded at` metadata strip with a compact refresh action, only surfaces a larger warning when the page becomes stale, and uses an approve/reject decision modal with system-vs-found values and impact copy. Approving a correctable finding now checks whether the master asset changed after the finding was reported; if current master data differs from the expected snapshot, the API returns a conflict warning and requires explicit confirmation before applying the correction. No database schema or assignment/closure/SOD behavior changed in this phase.
- Audit result Excel export guards worksheet date formatting by existing column keys only, preventing ExcelJS `Out of bounds. Excel supports columns from 1 to 16384` errors on result sheets that do not contain every audit/finding date column.
- Asset create/edit, clone, and batch create ownership fields now explicitly represent the asset owner/tag scope. The company and branch drive generated asset tags and reporting scope, while the custodian can be selected from a different company by enabling the cross-company custodian option. The custodian dropdown is scoped by selected company, branch, and department by default, still tolerates legacy employee rows whose `branchId` points to a stale/duplicate branch record when company and branch code match, warns when the selected custodian belongs to a different company, and writes `custodianScope` audit metadata for single and batch asset creation. Batch create keeps the row grid compact by default and exposes optional row-level override columns only when needed: custodian, department, current location, home location, and remark. Empty row override fields fall back to the shared batch values, which keeps normal same-site batches fast while supporting camera/device batches spread across many locations. When editing an existing asset whose saved custodian is already outside the owner scope, the form auto-enables cross-company custodian mode on load so the saved custodian remains visible instead of appearing blank. Asset Register and Asset Detail expose a clone action that opens `/assets/new?cloneFrom={assetId}`; the create form preloads shared master, ownership, location, purchase, warranty, custom-field, and purchase-document data from the source asset while leaving Asset Tag, Serial Number, and FA/accounting code blank, defaults the new record to Ready when that active status exists, shows a clone-context warning banner, and hides batch mode for that clone session.
- Asset create/edit now keeps Save and Cancel visible in a fixed bottom action bar across long single-asset forms. The bar preserves the existing duplicate/protected-status/saving disabled guards, stays aligned with the dashboard content area on desktop, and adds bottom form padding so the final fields remain visible above the action bar on mobile and desktop.
- Asset create/edit and batch create also separate owner/tag scope from physical location scope. Location dropdowns default to the selected owner branch, but operators can enable the cross-branch location option when the asset belongs to one company/branch while the real home/current location is another site. Cross-branch location choices show an explicit warning, and the dropdown labels append the branch label in cross mode so repeated location codes such as `FL2` remain distinguishable.
- Asset Check-in keeps the normal return workflow tied to active `AssetCheckout` rows, but now also supports legacy/imported assets that already have `Asset.custodianId` without an open checkout. The check-in page shows those legacy candidates in a searchable "ข้อมูลเก่า" panel with result counts and empty-state feedback. Operators create an auditable backfilled handover first (`/api/assets/{id}/legacy-checkout`, action `legacy_return_backfill`), then continue through the standard check-in form so return documents, movement history, lifecycle guards, and audit logs stay consistent.
- Asset Transfer now derives status server-side when custody moves to a person: providing `toCustodianId` sets the asset to `In Use` automatically, so operators do not need to remember a separate status edit. Location-only or department-only transfers preserve the current status. The review dialog shows the resulting status, and transfer movement/audit snapshots retain the before/after `statusId` alongside location, custodian, and department changes.
- Asset Import/Export history at `/th/asset-management/import-export` reads recent `system_logs` import batches, shows rollback-plan preview assets, and formats the hidden asset count through the `next-intl` `count` variable at render time. Keep placeholder-based messages such as `importHistoryMoreAssets` formatted with values instead of passing them as static label strings.
- Cross-scope asset visibility Phase 1 is reporting/listing only. Asset Register quick filters now expose assets where the custodian is in a different company, the custodian is in a different branch, or the current location belongs to a different branch than the owner/tag scope. Dashboard now surfaces the same active-asset cross-scope counts as a KPI, urgent-work card, and preview panel with drilldowns back to `/assets?crossScope=...`; Reports shows the same cross-scope summary/preview, `/api/assets/export` honors the same filter and adds owner/custodian/home/current location branch columns plus human-readable flags, and `/api/reports/assets-overview/export` includes cross-scope metrics and a Cross Scope sheet. This does not change custody, transfer, checkout/checkin, or asset master edit workflow rules.
- Performance Phase 1-2 is implemented for 2,000-5,000 asset scale: `prisma/schema.prisma` now maps hot-path indexes for Asset Register filters/sorts, cross-scope reporting, audit/finding queues, maintenance/disposal lists, evidence lookups, and system logs; `prisma/manual-migrations/2026-06-12-add-performance-indexes.sql` is already recorded as applied for `asset_management` and remains the idempotent SQL Server script for a different existing database after backup/approval. Cross-scope summary loading now fetches minimal candidate fields first and loads full display rows only for the bounded preview list. Dashboard uses a dedicated SQL Server aggregate path via `buildDashboardAssetCrossScopeSummary()` so `/dashboard` no longer pulls every active cross-scope candidate into Node just to render KPI/card/preview counts; Reports and exports continue to use the full summary path where complete row coverage is required.
- Performance timing instrumentation is available for menu/page latency diagnosis without changing business logic. Set `PERFORMANCE_TIMING=1` or `PERFORMANCE_LOGGING=1` in the server environment to emit `[performance] {"event":"performance_timing",...}` log lines around high-latency server data groups on Dashboard, Asset Register, Asset Detail, Reports, Audit Rounds, and Audit Scan. Current labels include `dashboard.initial-data` plus Dashboard subgroups `dashboard.kpi-counts`, `dashboard.recent-activity`, `dashboard.urgent-work`, `dashboard.approval-inbox`, `dashboard.cross-scope`, and `dashboard.monthly-trends`; Asset/Register labels `assets.initial-data`, `assets.model-photos`; Asset Detail labels `asset-detail.initial-data`, `asset-detail.relationship-data`, `asset-detail.evidence-data`, `asset-detail.operation-data`; Reports labels `reports.shared-data` plus the active view's one label from `reports.overview-data`, `reports.accounting-data`, `reports.operations-data`, or `reports.catalog-data`; Audit labels `audit-rounds.initial-data`, `audit-rounds.progress-data`, `audit-scan.initial-data`, and `audit-scan.checklist-data`; and since phase 2B plan 5 the admin labels `admin-logs.list-data` (the system log's page of rows and its count) and `approval-history.list-data` (the approval history's rows, count and decision totals). Keep the flag off during normal operation unless diagnosing slow navigation.
- Page-level skeleton loading is available for slow authenticated routes without changing page data logic. `src/components/ui/page-skeleton.tsx` provides shared shape-matching skeletons, `src/app/[locale]/(dashboard)/loading.tsx` covers the authenticated dashboard shell fallback, and route-specific fallbacks now exist for `/dashboard`, `/assets`, `/assets/{id}`, and `/reports`. These skeletons use muted panels/cards/table rows instead of spinner-only loading so slow navigation still feels responsive while server-rendered data resolves. Future slow routes should reuse the shared skeleton component rather than adding one-off spinners. Since phase 2B plan 4 each skeleton takes the shape of its page at full width (`DESIGN.md` Adaptive UI Contract), and the shell fallback is a header over a table.
- Asset Register preserves the operator's current list context when drilling into records. Detail, edit, and clone links append a sanitized `returnTo` target for the current `/assets` URL, Asset Detail exposes an explicit Back action, and Asset Form save/cancel flows return to the original page/filter/sort/search state instead of resetting to page 1.
- Asset Register desktop workspace memory is browser-local and does not change API, schema, or permission behavior. A bare `/{locale}/assets` route restores the latest saved filter, sort, and page-size preference for that locale; an explicit query-string URL remains authoritative and updates that saved preference. Pagination is never restored automatically. Column visibility continues to use its existing browser-local preference. Opening an Asset Detail from the register stores the dashboard scroll position only for that exact `returnTo` URL and restores it once when the operator returns. The shared `ActionEmptyState` now supplies the baseline empty, error, and permission-denied surface for Asset Register, dashboard errors, and access-denied; future list/error pages should reuse it rather than recreate one-off panels. Manual UAT: filter/sort/page-size, open a detail row, use Back, then confirm the list context and scroll are restored; direct shared URLs should not be replaced.
- UI hardening follow-up keeps all existing business workflows and APIs intact. General Asset Scan now carries an allowlisted `returnTo` when it opens Asset Detail, so the Detail Back action returns to the same scanner instead of the register. Shared searchable selects support Arrow keys, Home/End, Enter, Escape, focus return, and disabled-option skipping; their clear action is a real button. The shared confirmation dialog replaces browser prompts for affected audit/component actions and traps/returns focus correctly. My Assets now has a server-scoped read-only detail route (`/{locale}/my-assets/{id}`) that queries only the signed-in employee's custody and omits purchase, supplier, accounting, disposal, audit, and admin-only data. System Settings keeps the selected tab in the URL and registers a browser leave warning only while unsaved changes exist. Asset Detail and Reports now have route-specific skeleton fallbacks in addition to Dashboard and Asset Register. All of these are UI/accessibility/navigation changes only; RBAC, SOD, audit logging, schemas, scanner decoding, and save semantics are unchanged.
- UI/UX operational pass further separates overview from daily work without changing business behavior: Work Center opens approval/maintenance/audit/disposal queues first, while Asset Data is an explicit panel; Asset Detail has URL-backed reading presets and checkout/check-in/transfer review dialogs; Maintenance keeps its filters in the URL (the board view was removed with the 2026-10-07 repair records); Disposal rows show a clear pending/approved-awaiting-execution/completed stage with one permission-aware next action; Reports has named browser-local filter presets; Settings searches setting keys/descriptions and links changed values back to their tab; and Audit Scan remembers a browser-local Location/Department work area per round for queue/progress presentation only. Master Data lists rely on the application Sidebar/mobile More menu instead of repeating a partial workspace strip inside each page. `ActionEmptyState` remains the common empty/error/permission-denied surface. These are UI/navigation/local-preference changes only: APIs, schema, RBAC, SOD, audit history, data lifecycle, and scanner save behavior are unchanged.
- Operational list workflows now preserve context beyond Asset Register. Repair record detail/print links, Disposal request detail/print links, Audit Round detail/pending/scan links, and Audit Findings disposal follow-up links carry a sanitized internal `returnTo` target so Back/cancel style navigation returns operators to the originating filtered list or round detail instead of resetting to the default page. The shared helper only accepts allowlisted internal workflow paths for maintenance, disposal, audit rounds, audit findings, and audit round detail pages.
- Maintenance keeps repair records and PM plans apart. Repair records are created at `/maintenance/new`, from Asset Detail ("บันทึกซ่อม"), from the "PM ถึงกำหนด" box ("บันทึกว่าทำแล้ว", saved with `maintenancePlanId`), or by check-in "ส่งซ่อม". `PATCH /api/maintenance-tickets/{id}` takes `action: "complete" | "cancel" | "update"`, each with `expectedUpdatedAt`; API clients receive stable codes (`MAINTENANCE_ASSET_WRITTEN_OFF`, `MAINTENANCE_ASSET_ON_LOAN`, `MAINTENANCE_OPEN_RECORD_EXISTS`, `MAINTENANCE_ASSET_INELIGIBLE`, `MAINTENANCE_REPORTER_REQUIRED`, `MAINTENANCE_INVALID_TRANSITION`, `MAINTENANCE_CONFLICT`) translated under `maintenancePage.errors`. `/maintenance` lists records with status/date/search/asset filters, the PM due box, and the "ทรัพย์สินค้างสถานะซ่อมแต่ไม่มีบันทึก" box; master-data option search stays bounded. PM plans (`/maintenance/pm`) distinguish reversible `paused` from terminal `ended` and retain inactive plans for audit. Both `2026-07-14-add-maintenance-plan-ticket-link.sql` and `2026-07-14-add-maintenance-plan-state.sql` are already recorded as applied for `asset_management`; `2026-10-07-add-maintenance-outcome.sql` is not yet. A different existing SQL Server database must apply all three after backup/approval, then run `npx prisma generate`, build, and restart before deploying the new code.
- Asset Register now supports high-volume operational views directly on `/th/assets`: URL-backed quick filters cover all assets, missing Serial, missing photo, purchase-info gaps, warranty expiring, missing responsibility, Ready, In Use, Checked Out, Pending Repair, and Under Maintenance while preserving the existing search/company/branch/category context. Those quick filters are grouped by data quality, ownership/custody scope, and lifecycle status; on mobile the groups render as a horizontal strip so the first asset cards stay closer to the top. The register search box also matches the current custodian's employee code (`Employee.code`) in addition to asset identifiers and custodian name, but intentionally does not search repair reporters, assignees, or other related employees. The Import Wizard remains collapsed but now sits after the register table/card list so daily lookup work is prioritized. The table defaults to the `Operations` preset before stored browser preferences load, persists `All`, `Operations`, `Accounting`, and `Audit` per browser, and keeps column/export/template controls desktop-only while mobile cards focus on lookup and row actions. Active filters now summarize search/company/branch/category/status/condition/ownership/data-quality/cross-scope/master-data drilldowns in one removable panel with a clear-all action. Desktop table UX keeps Asset Tag/name sticky on the left, actions sticky on the right, shows a horizontal-scroll hint, clamps long asset names to two readable lines with the full name in the title, and gives whole-row navigation a visible keyboard focus ring.
- Asset Register now accepts master-data drilldown filters from Category and Brand/Model counts. Category model counts open the Brand/Model workspace scoped to that category, category asset counts open `/assets?categoryId=...`, brand asset counts open `/assets?brandId=...`, and model asset counts open `/assets?modelId=...`. Active Brand/Model drilldowns show removable filter chips above the register filters and are preserved through the filter form. The register filter panel keeps search/company/branch/category as primary filters, moves status/condition/ownership/page-size into a collapsible advanced section that auto-opens when any advanced filter is active, and keeps lifecycle/condition help icons compact inside the advanced labels.
- Master Data usability now presents the Brand / Model page as a compact left-side brand navigator plus a right-side model workspace. Create Model is the primary header action, Create Brand remains available, duplicate review is compacted, the brand navigator uses a narrower desktop column so the model table has more usable width, and brand navigator counts are derived from active `AssetModel`/`Asset` group counts so soft-deleted models do not make the sidebar disagree with the visible model table. Supplier master data labels the existing `Supplier.code` field as `Tax ID / Supplier Code` (`เลขประจำตัวผู้เสียภาษี / รหัสผู้ขาย`) for Thai vendor workflows while keeping legacy/internal vendor code compatibility and the existing 20-character unique column.
- Supplier management hardening keeps routine edits independent from relationship protection: PUT permits contact/profile updates for referenced suppliers, but an active-to-inactive transition and DELETE both load active asset, maintenance-ticket, PM-plan, and purchase-document counts and reject the lifecycle change when any count is nonzero. The list summary uses bounded count queries, renders operational cards below `md` and a semantic sortable table from `md`, removes the redundant always-active column, and provides actionable filtered/unfiltered empty states. Create/edit mirrors database length limits, normalizes optional blanks, maps validation/duplicate failures to localized inline fields, warns on dirty navigation, and uses 44px controls. Supplier detail uses a mobile metric strip and labelled purchase-document cards below `md`, retaining the table on larger screens. No database migration is required.
- Asset create and batch create now auto-select an asset model when the selected category and brand uniquely match one active model, so model-level photos can appear in the asset list/detail even when the user does not manually pick the model. Asset Register thumbnails prefer the active model photo (`attachments.module=asset_model`) first and fall back to the latest asset-specific photo (`attachments.module=asset`) only when the model has no photo, keeping list views visually consistent by model while preserving asset-specific photos for detail/evidence workflows. Category master data respects SQL Server unique category codes with soft delete: recreating a deleted category reactivates the inactive row, delete/deactivation is blocked when assets or models still reference the category, and active category custom-field templates can still be edited without deleting the category. Category create now uses a create-only nested custom-field payload while update/reactivate uses the replace payload, preventing Prisma `deleteMany` from being sent to `assetCategory.create()`.
- Asset Detail photo/evidence UX separates register photos from historical evidence. The right-rail model/asset preview cards stay compact, `รูปทรัพย์สิน` remains the working area for asset/model photos, checklist photos, and direct register attachments, and the full cross-workflow Evidence Center is now opened from a compact drawer action instead of a duplicate page section. The unified event timeline remains the primary place to understand when purchase, custody, component, repair, audit, or disposal events happened; the evidence drawer is only the file index/filter surface.
- Reports branch breakdown now labels branches with the owning company code (`Company / Branch - Name`) so repeated branch codes/names across companies remain distinguishable in `/th/reports`. Shared report tables also avoid using label-only React keys, preventing duplicate-key console warnings when two grouped rows legitimately have the same display label.
- Accounting depreciation policy is edited through a structured Settings UI instead of raw JSON as the primary workflow. The builder manages default useful life, default residual value, category-specific policy groups, a purchase-date-based calculation preview, and an advanced JSON fallback while preserving the existing `accounting_depreciation_policy` setting shape. Depreciation still starts from `Asset.purchaseDate` in this phase.
- System Settings > Asset Numbering now manages Asset Tag Prefix by prefix group instead of one row per category. Operators enter a prefix, move categories from available to selected lists with search, see existing-prefix badges when a category is assigned elsewhere, and save back to the existing `asset_tag_category_prefixes` JSON shape (`categoryId -> prefix`) so asset tag generation behavior and database schema stay unchanged. The grouping UI filters stale/inactive category ids out of the visible assigned/unassigned counts, while editing or removing a prefix group cleans up stale rows for that prefix.
- Storage Governance includes an archive action column for orphan-file dry-run actions. `storagePage.action` is now covered in Thai/English messages, and `tests/storage-archive-ui.test.ts` checks every `storagePage` key used by the page and archive button to prevent missing-message regressions.
- Asset lifecycle hardening now allows checkout only from Ready and personal transfer only from Ready/In Use, returns corrective maintenance to custody-derived In Use/Ready or Pending Disposal, limits disposal execution to Disposed/Retired, and lets protected lifecycle status corrections restore accidental Pending Disposal/Disposed/Retired/Lost/Missing/Under Maintenance/Pending Repair/Under Inspection statuses back to Ready with a required reason and audit trail. Asset create/edit also blocks direct protected lifecycle status changes in the form before submit and points operators to the proper workflow, such as recording a repair instead of setting a repair status by hand. Keep status and condition separate: status controls workflow availability, while condition records the physical state and should guide whether the next action is Ready, repair, disposal, lost/missing follow-up, or correction. A saveable lifecycle reference diagram is available at `docs/asset-lifecycle-flow.png` with the SVG source at `docs/asset-lifecycle-flow.svg`.
- Asset status/condition guidance is now visible inline through help icons on Asset Create/Edit, Asset Detail, Asset Register filters, and Asset Register table headers. The popovers explain lifecycle status versus physical condition, protected workflow expectations, and normal next actions so operators can decide whether to use Ready, maintenance, disposal, lost/missing follow-up, or status correction without leaving the working page.
- Production readiness now tracks LDAP sync and Notification Digest scheduler run status (PM generation was removed on 2026-10-07; the scheduler token check counts 2 tokens), and upload scanner configuration separately. Upload hardening now includes content signature validation, an optional server-side scanner hook, central browser security headers, and no-sniff/private attachment responses in addition to size, MIME, and extension checks.
- LDAP/AD login auto-provision now links the app user to an active Employee matched by LDAP email or `employeeID` before creating the user record. This avoids SQL Server's unique `users.employeeId` behavior with duplicate `NULL` values and keeps AD users tied to employee master data. `LDAP_DEFAULT_ROLE` must still point to an existing active role such as `employee`.
- Dashboard RBAC UX now filters sidebar navigation from the signed-in user's permissions before rendering, keeps `system_admin` unrestricted, and sends direct unauthorized page hits to `/{locale}/access-denied` instead of a generic 404. The topbar avatar/name also uses the current session user instead of hard-coded Admin text. System Settings > AD/LDAP Login loads active roles from the database for the default role searchable selector, so operators do not need to memorize role keys.
- Employee self-service asset visibility is available at `/{locale}/my-assets`. The page is scoped by the signed-in user's linked `employeeId`, does not require broad `asset:view`, shows only active assets where the employee is the current custodian, and allows thumbnails only for those owned asset attachments. Linked employee users without overview-module permissions now land on My Assets after login, and direct `/dashboard` hits redirect there before dashboard-wide KPI queries run.
- Login uses the shared Light Modern Enterprise tokens and real application icon while preserving the existing Local-first, AD/LDAP-fallback credential flow. Visible labels are associated with inputs, username/current-password autocomplete metadata supports password managers, and the password visibility control uses localized Lucide Eye/EyeOff actions. Failed authentication keeps the username, clears/refocuses the password, and exposes a generic live error. Optional `callbackUrl` values return users to a same-origin, same-locale route after login; external, protocol-relative, backslash, cross-locale, array, and malformed values fall back to the role-aware `/{locale}` entry. `reason=session-expired` and `error=SessionExpired` expose the existing session-expired notice. This change does not add remember-me, password reset, MFA, provider selection, or change the eight-hour session policy.
- Login UX verification completed on 2026-07-11 on `codex/login-ux`. Focused login/navigation tests passed 16/16, `npm test` passed 767/767, and `npm run verify` completed lint, tests, Prisma generation, TypeScript, and the Next.js 16.2.4 production build with 54/54 static pages. Authenticated-free browser QA at 390x844 and 1440x900 confirmed no body overflow, associated labels, 44px mobile inputs/actions, 16px mobile input text, working Eye/EyeOff focus retention, explicit session-expired status, password clearing/refocus after invalid credentials, and no console errors. Production-like password-manager behavior and a valid AD/LDAP callback-return login still require manual UAT.
- Dashboard shell scroll ownership is now explicit: `src/app/[locale]/(dashboard)/layout.tsx` is fixed to the viewport with `fixed inset-0`, while the `<main>` content area owns vertical scrolling. This prevents the browser document scrollbar and the in-app content scrollbar from appearing side by side on dashboard pages. `DashboardShell` also guards hash-anchor navigation such as Asset Detail `#notes` / `#audit`: if the browser tries to scroll the outer fixed shell, the shell is reset to `0` and the target section is scrolled inside `<main>` instead, preventing large blank tail space from sticking at the bottom of the viewport.
- Mobile Responsive QA completed across five phases. Phase 1 completed the responsive foundation for shared panels, action rows, dashboard shell, topbar/sidebar touch targets, global search containment, and mobile-safe topbar popovers. Phase 2 improved field workflows for QR/barcode scanner input, searchable selects, asset scan/search, single and batch asset create, checkout/checkin/transfer forms, audit scan camera/manual fallback, and asset-detail quick actions. Phase 3 improved data-heavy pages: Asset Register now uses mobile cards below `md`, audit rounds/detail/findings stack actions and cards, maintenance/disposal list and detail workflows use mobile cards and touch-safe action modals, reports/work center contain table scroll and action rows, and admin settings/readiness controls wrap safely. Phase 4 made `/th/asset-management/labels` and label print routes mobile-safe by stacking toolbar actions, containing preview overflow, and scaling screen preview separately from the configured Brother tape print size. Phase 5 polished touch targets and focus-visible behavior for shared controls, scanner input, asset forms, audit scan evidence removal, maintenance/disposal modals, and audit finding dialogs. Baseline and after screenshots were captured for dashboard at `375/390/414/768px`; unauthenticated protected routes were checked for redirect overflow at `375px`. Authenticated field-workflow, data-heavy, and label print routes should still be tested on a real mobile device for camera permission, file upload/capture, signature pad, long Thai content, scan timing, and the Brother print dialog.
- Before production release, complete the UAT checklist by role, production readiness checklist, security review, backup/restore test, and deployment verification.
- Run `npm run verify` and `npm run build` before release or handoff.

## Core Verification Commands

```powershell
npm install
npx prisma generate
npm run verify
npm run build
```

## Production Safety Rules

- Local development uses `asset_management_dev` with the `asset_dev` login (db_owner on that database only). Never put `sa` or the Production `asset_management` database in a developer `.env`; run `npm run migration:status` before starting to confirm `Database: asset_management_dev`.
- Do not commit real credentials, production connection strings, internal hostnames, or server IP addresses.
- Do not document real production admin credentials. Initial admin accounts are for local seed/testing only and must be changed before shared or production use.
- Do not hard-delete production data except through explicitly guarded cleanup tooling that requires scope, apply flag, confirmation flag, and environment confirmation.
- Do not change core workflow behavior during handoff cleanup unless the change is separately reviewed and tested.
- Treat `npx prisma db push` as Dev/Test-only unless a production change record, backup, and rollback/restore plan are approved.

## Operational Notes

- For Integration API clients, use `Admin > Integration API` to create, edit display name/scopes, rotate, disable, and enable clients. The plain token is returned once on create/rotate and belongs only in the external system secret manager. Adding a scope affects the existing token immediately and requires confirmation in the UI. The client list operations panel reads existing `system_logs` to show usage/error/top-endpoint activity and can copy a scope-aware PowerShell example with a token placeholder. `npm run integration:token` remains available for controlled SQL recovery/troubleshooting only and does not write files. Emergency disable should use the UI or controlled SQL against `integration_api_clients.enabled`; if the table is removed, roll back code first.
- Before printing production Asset Labels, set `Public QR Base URL` in `Admin > Settings > Label / QR` to the permanent HTTPS domain, for example `https://asset.company.com`; the print page should show a full `/q/a/{assetId}` URL and no localhost/private warning.
- If category-prefixed suggested asset names are too long for the tape, use `Admin > Settings > Label / QR > ชื่อทรัพย์สินบน Label` and keep `ย่ออัตโนมัติ` selected. Switch to `ชื่อเต็ม` only when the label must print the exact master `Asset.name`; no separate label-name field is stored on the asset.
- For Brother PT-E550W with Laminated Tape 18mm, the app label profile is `18mm / 0.70"`. The Windows/Brother Printer Properties must also be set to Tape Size `18mm / 0.70"` before printing.
- Label text is optimized for thermal tape output with black, high-weight typography. If print quality is still faint, check Brother driver density/quality settings before changing app layout.
- On `/th/asset-management/labels`, use queue filters before selecting large batches: choose company or branch when printing a whole site/company batch, add location only when splitting a branch into smaller routes, then sort selected labels by Asset Tag, location, or category to match the physical labeling route. The "add by current filter" action follows that summarized scope. The current print-tracking model records print batches when the print page runs; a future enhancement may split "print requested" from "print confirmed" if operational proof of printer success is required.
- For mobile camera scanning, test `/th/asset-management/scan`, Serial Number scanner inputs, and `/th/audit/rounds/{id}/scan` on the target phone browser. Some browsers hide camera labels until permission is granted, so scanner helpers prefer a generic rear-camera/environment option where possible. `/th/asset-management/scan` intentionally scans Asset Label QR codes only, should default to the generic rear camera option, show an undistorted 4:3 camera preview with a smaller square white guidance frame, and should navigate to the asset detail automatically after a recognized QR scan. On supported devices, the existing preview should expose flashlight plus 1x, 2x, and 3x zoom controls; tapping 1x should return to the unzoomed preview without closing the camera, and unsupported devices should hide unavailable controls. The Asset QR camera requests high-resolution 4096x3072/30fps ideal constraints and then best-effort applies the camera track's maximum reported resolution; browsers may still choose a lower supported capture size. During UAT, center only the QR code in the frame and adjust distance until the QR modules are sharp; if the preview looks blurry, hold the label slightly farther away and use available zoom/focus tuning to enlarge the QR. Do not use the frame as a fit-box for the entire long label. `/th/audit/rounds/{id}/scan` uses the same native-resolution Asset QR decoder but stays on the audit workflow, defaults to rear-camera walking mode, continues scanning for the next label, selects the scanned asset, displays a readable Asset Tag/label in the scan input, and keeps the raw QR URL only as the latest QR reference. On supported devices, verify the existing audit camera preview exposes 1x, 2x, and 3x zoom buttons without adding a separate scan mode; tapping 1x should return to the unzoomed preview without closing the camera. Unsupported devices should hide the zoom buttons. Manual entry on Audit Scan supports partial suggestions after at least two characters and requires choosing a row when multiple matches exist; this must not change QR exact-match behavior. The sticky progress header is the only progress summary; the old metric cards are intentionally removed, and the normal camera-ready status panel stays hidden unless the camera is active, a decoded reference is available, or a camera error needs attention. After selection, verify the system data panel before choosing `บันทึกพบตรง` or `บันทึกข้อมูลไม่ตรง / Finding`; on mobile these actions are available in the bottom action bar together with `ถ่ายรูปหลักฐาน` and `สแกนต่อ / กรอกเอง`. If field data differs, use detailed mode and attach at least one evidence photo. Audit evidence photos are intentionally free-form and can be added multiple times with optional grouping tags. Use the Pending List/Zone Queue, not the successful scan result card, to mark an expected item as not found. Use the Serial Number fields when collecting manufacturer barcodes; center the barcode band on the scan line, make the bars fill most of the horizontal frame, keep fingers out of the quiet zone, and adjust distance until the vertical lines look sharp. Serial scanner crop fallback reads crop `ImageData` directly through ZXing luminance/binarizer primitives instead of passing a canvas to the browser reader, which is important on mobile browsers where the bundled reader only captures video/image elements reliably.
- Asset Detail relationship map is a read-only structural view, not the install/remove editor. It uses three lanes (`Installed under` / current item / `Has components`) plus a status summary to clarify whether the viewed asset is a parent asset, a component, both, or standalone. Desktop retains the three-lane map; mobile presents the same parent/current/child meaning as a vertical flow with directional connectors. Relationship cards are fully clickable and keep the Asset Tag readable before the asset name and role badge. Child relationships show five rows initially and reveal the remainder on demand without dropping data.
- Asset Detail uses one URL-backed tab row: `ภาพรวม`, `การถือครอง`, `การดำเนินการ`, and `ตรวจนับ`. Each tab renders only its relevant information group; it does not change asset data, responsibility/custody data, permissions, or lifecycle workflow. On mobile the active tab scrolls into view, the row uses snap alignment, and an edge cue indicates additional tabs. Header actions are permission-filtered, the mobile back icon has an accessible label, and the bottom action bar adapts to the number of visible lifecycle actions instead of reserving four empty columns. Since phase 2B plan 4 the tab row is the shared `TabNav` (one underline at every width, the custody count in plain digits, the missing-serial warning before its label) inside `AssetDetailTabs`, which keeps the scrolling, the snap, the current tab in view and the edge cue; the back button is `PageBackLink`, named by its visible words.
- Asset Detail first-viewport hierarchy answers identity, status, condition, current location, and current responsibility in one compact header. The old repeated summary-card facts are removed from Overview. `สิ่งที่ต้องติดตาม` renders only when there is an actionable exception and keeps the complete data-quality checklist behind one expandable detail surface. Audit activity and table rows use localized status/result labels instead of raw workflow enums. Ordinary Asset Detail reads use bounded, active-view history limits; a separate ID-only evidence reference index keeps the cross-workflow Evidence Center complete. Since phase 2B plan 4 that header is `PageHeader`: the asset name is the `h1`, and the code, the status and condition badges with their help, the location and the custodian sit in its meta row.
- Asset Detail UX polish verification completed on 2026-07-11 on `codex/asset-detail-ux-polish`. Focused navigation/action/relationship/data-boundary tests passed 30/30; `npx tsc --noEmit` passed; `npm test` passed 757/757; `npm run build` and `npm run verify` completed with Next.js 16.2.4 and 54/54 static pages. Lint completed with 0 errors and 256 inherited warnings from bundled `.agents`/`.gemini` skill tooling. Authenticated Chrome QA at 390x844, 768x1024, and 1440x900 confirmed no body horizontal overflow, adaptive two-action mobile layout for the tested role, one tab row with mobile overflow affordance, and equivalent vertical-mobile/three-lane-desktop relationship meaning. A browser-discovered `MISSING_MESSAGE` regression was fixed by using the existing `auditRound` translation namespace and reloading with no new console errors. Real-device safe-area/font-scale checks and limited-role action combinations still require UAT.
- The custody tab keeps a compact, read-only component summary. Users with `asset:edit` open `จัดการส่วนควบ` at `/[locale]/assets/{assetId}/components`, then scan or search at least two characters, enter installation details, review, and confirm using the existing component API/audit/evidence workflow. The manager returns to the same asset custody tab. View-only users can inspect the relationship summary and manager history but cannot install or remove a component. Candidate lookup is intentionally empty below two characters, and component history is revealed in small batches.
- A repair record saved as finished stores its record date as `returnDate`; an unfinished record leaves `returnDate` empty until "ซ่อมเสร็จ" supplies it. An unfinished record accepts `Ready`/`In Use`, plus legacy `Pending Repair`; `Checked Out` assets must be returned first, and `beyond_repair` is refused while any active checkout exists. A finished record may be saved for any asset that is not Disposed/Retired; an asset stuck in `Pending Repair`/`Under Maintenance` without an unfinished record then returns to its custody-derived status. If an asset is currently `Under Inspection`, complete the controlled inspection correction to `Ready` with a reason before starting personal transfer, maintenance, or disposal. Audit Mark Not Found creates an investigation finding but does not automatically set `Missing`/`Lost`; a dedicated loss-confirmation workflow remains future work.
- Repair record updates separate status from details. The record detail has one "ซ่อมเสร็จ" action (`action: "complete"`: return date, outcome, optional vendor/cost/invoice/remark/files), one "ยกเลิกบันทึก" action (`action: "cancel"`, unfinished records only), and "แก้ไขรายละเอียด" (`action: "update"`: date, problem, vendor, cost, invoice number, remark). Detail edits never change `repairStatus`, `outcome`, or the asset status; a wrong outcome is fixed with Status Correction plus a note in the record's remark. All three use `expectedUpdatedAt` and return `MAINTENANCE_CONFLICT` on a stale write; finishing or cancelling a record that is no longer unfinished returns `MAINTENANCE_INVALID_TRANSITION`. Records created from a PM plan follow the same asset rules as any other record; there is no separate PM work-order path any more.
- There are no waiting states, planning fields, SLA, close checklist, evidence lock, or maintenance close approval in the new workflow. Old values in `assignedToId`, `dueDate`, `laborCost`, `partsCost`, `quotationNo`, `warrantyClaim`, `rootCause`, and `inspectedById` stay in the database and show read-only under "ข้อมูลจากขั้นตอนเดิม" on the record detail. Attachments may be added at any time (`maintenance:edit`, or `maintenance:create` on the user's own record) and removed with `maintenance:edit` (soft delete with a System Log entry), including after the record is finished.

Mobile now uses explicit card/list hooks and prioritizes Asset Tag plus semantic status, name, serial/category metadata, location, custodian, field-relevant condition/shared-ownership warnings, then detail/edit actions; company/branch and purchase price remain available through the desktop table and asset detail. Clone and delete actions are retained under the mobile card's secondary expandable action area.
- Controller browser QA and review completed with the `system_admin` session. At 375, 390, and 414 pixels, the register showed the mobile card list only with 25 sample cards, no body/list/card horizontal overflow, readable text status, and the intended hierarchy; company/branch and purchase price were omitted. The secondary disclosure opened, and its full-width summary measured 44px high. The filtered-empty mobile state showed `ไม่พบข้อมูล` without overflow.
- At 768, 1280, and 1440 pixels, the dense desktop table was the only register data view and the body did not overflow. Mobile search rendered before quick filters (top 249 versus 568 at 375); desktop preserved quick filters before the form (top 185 versus 447 at 1280).
- Runtime QA caught and corrected a missing canonical status/condition projection. Status and condition tones now use explicit, exhaustively tested mappings. Reviewer approval followed 651/651 full tests, 35/35 focused tests, scoped ESLint, `tsc --noEmit`, and a production build.

## Adaptive Reports Milestone

- `/{locale}/reports` is now one URL-backed reporting workspace with `view=overview|accounting|operations|catalog`; a missing or unsupported value safely opens Overview. Supported asset filters remain in the URL when changing views. The shared order is header actions, scrollable view navigation, filter panel, removable active-filter chips, two compact shared metrics, and only the selected view's content.
- Overview contains the latest matching assets and core status/category/company/branch/department/ownership breakdowns. Accounting contains the filtered cost, repair, depreciation, and net-book-value datasets. Operations contains actionable data-quality, cross-scope, custody/location, frequent-repair, and idle-asset insight. Catalog separates browser-local asset-report presets from module-owned recurring exports and states that Maintenance, Disposal, and Audit exports do not inherit the asset filters above.
- Reports and Asset Register now share the supported `activity=idle_180d` query contract. Operations data-quality and cross-scope links preserve compatible filters, frequent-repair grouping is constrained by the selected asset scope, and the idle count/drilldown use the same no-movement-in-180-days predicate. Removing Company also removes its dependent Branch filter.
- The adaptive presentation keeps semantic tables at `md` and above and labelled card lists below `md` for wide datasets. Mobile keeps filters before metrics, full-width permission-aware header actions where needed, horizontally scrollable tabs, removable chips, and 44px controls; the dashboard `<main>` remains the intended vertical scroll owner.
- Final release verification on 2026-07-15 passed 1,088/1,088 tests, scoped ESLint, standalone `npx tsc --noEmit`, the scoped Impeccable detector with `[]`, and `git diff --check`. A fresh `npm run build` generated Prisma Client 7.8.0, compiled with Next.js 16.2.4, completed TypeScript, and generated 59/59 static pages after loading the ignored main-checkout environment into that build process only. The build emitted only the known linked-worktree multiple-lockfile root warning.
- Authenticated Chrome QA used `system_admin` across all four views at 375/390/414/768/1280/1440. Every combination retained a single document scroll owner, no nested vertical scroller, no body/main/card horizontal overflow, one view content region, and an active tab identified by `aria-current` plus an icon. Mobile rendered cards only with no action below 44px; `md+` rendered semantic tables only. At 375x812, the first Overview breakdown began at 5.9 viewport heights after the browser-found density and Operations touch-target defects were fixed. A direct 375px Catalog load now uses the snap strip to reveal the active tab, and applying visible filters retains URL-only Brand/Model/Custodian/Supplier/Data Quality/Cross-scope/Activity scopes. All supported Data Quality and Cross-scope chip values are localized, while blocked browser-storage access or writes now report a localized preset-save failure instead of throwing. Company/Branch/Status/Data Quality tab persistence and dependent Company/Branch removal passed. A temporary local preset stored `view=catalog` with its Company filter and was removed after inspection. Catalog module-filter copy was present, and no new console error/warning appeared after the QA start time.

Operations generated exact filtered data-quality and `activity=idle_180d` hrefs, but browser safety policy blocked automated destination navigation, so full drilldown/export reconciliation remains open together with an `accounting` or other permission-limited role smoke test, real-device checks, and production-like repeated-branch/cross-scope reconciliation. Do not mark `docs/08_PRODUCTION_READINESS.md`'s realistic-data Reports/export checkbox complete from this controller evidence.
